# The exact reconstruction threshold for the three-state symmetric channel

{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We determine the exact reconstruction threshold for the symmetric three-state broadcast process on every regular <i>b</i>-ary tree, <i>b</i> ≥ 2, and every observed Poisson Galton–Watson tree of mean <i>d</i> &gt; 1. Reconstruction occurs exactly when $`d\\lambda^2\\gt 1`$, with <i>d</i> = <i>b</i> in the regular model; there is non-reconstruction at equality for either sign of the channel parameter. The Poisson advantage is averaged over trees and spins without conditioning on survival. This resolves the all-degree three-state regular-tree prediction. Combining the Poisson theorem with known tree-to-graph and algorithmic results gives the exact weak-recovery threshold for the symmetric three-community sparse stochastic block model with independent uniform labels, fixed within- and between-community rates $`a,b\\gt 0`$, and mean degree $`(a+2b)/3\\gt 1`$: recovery is possible exactly when $`(a-b)^2\\gt 3(a+2b)`$. Above this threshold it is achievable in $`O(n\\log n)`$ time; at or below it, weak recovery is information-theoretically impossible.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We determine the exact reconstruction threshold for the symmetric three-state broadcast process on every regular <i>b</i>-ary tree, <i>b</i> ≥ 2, and every observed Poisson Galton–Watson tree of mean <i>d</i> &gt; 1. Reconstruction occurs exactly when $`d\lambda^2\gt 1`$, with <i>d</i> = <i>b</i> in the regular model; there is non-reconstruction at equality for either sign of the channel parameter. The Poisson advantage is averaged over trees and spins without conditioning on survival. This resolves the all-degree three-state regular-tree prediction. Combining the Poisson theorem with known tree-to-graph and algorithmic results gives the exact weak-recovery threshold for the symmetric three-community sparse stochastic block model with independent uniform labels, fixed within- and between-community rates $`a,b\gt 0`$, and mean degree $`(a+2b)/3\gt 1`$: recovery is possible exactly when $`(a-b)^2\gt 3(a+2b)`$. Above this threshold it is achievable in $`O(n\log n)`$ time; at or below it, weak recovery is information-theoretically impossible.

- **claim-exact-threshold** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-threshold)): For every integer $b\ge2$ in the regular model and every real $d>1$ in the Poisson model, reconstruction occurs if and only if $$d\lambda^2>1,$$ with $d=b$ in the regular case. At every admissible equality $d\lambda^2=1$, there is non-reconstruction.
  - Explanation: On regular and Poisson trees, reconstruction of the three-state symmetric broadcast holds exactly when the second-moment condition is strict. At equality there is non-reconstruction for either sign of the channel parameter.
- **claim-sbm-weak-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-sbm)): In the model above, the following statements hold.* 1. *If $(a-b)^2\le 3(a+2b)$, then every sequence of estimators $\widehat\sigma_n$ using only $G_n$ and independent randomization satisfies $$\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb E\operatorname{ov}(\sigma,\widehat\sigma_n)=\frac13.$$* 2. *If $(a-b)^2>3(a+2b)$, there are $\varepsilon>0$ and an algorithm, allowed to depend on the fixed parameters $a,b$, which runs in $O(n\log n)$ time and satisfies $$\mathbb P\!\left(\operatorname{ov}(\sigma,\widehat\sigma_n) \ge\frac13+\varepsilon\right)\longrightarrow1.$$* *Thus weak recovery is possible exactly under the strict inequality, and is information-theoretically impossible at equality.
  - Explanation: For the symmetric three-community sparse block model, weak recovery is possible exactly above the threshold, with a near-linear-time algorithm. At or below it, no estimator beats the random-guess overlap.
- **claim-ordered-unlabelled** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fnd-ordered)): The ordered and unlabelled depth observations are equivalent for inference of the root spin. In particular, they have the same reconstruction advantage and the same distribution of the root posterior.
  - Explanation: Forgetting the sibling order loses no information about the root spin, because the conditional law of the order does not depend on the root spin.
- **claim-tv-second-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fnd-norms)): For every posterior vector, $$\label{fnd:tv-pointwise} \frac{\|\zeta\|}{\sqrt3} \leq\left\\|p-\operatorname{Unif}(\mathcal S)\right\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \leq\frac23\|\zeta\|.$$ Consequently, writing $\mu_\ell=\mathbb Ex_\ell$ for the depth experiment and $a_\ell$ for its mean total variation advantage, $$\label{fnd:tv-moment} \frac{\mu_\ell}{\sqrt3}\leq a_\ell \leq\frac23\sqrt{\mu_\ell}.$$ In particular, vanishing advantage is equivalent to vanishing posterior second moment.
  - Explanation: The total variation advantage is sandwiched between multiples of the posterior second moment, so vanishing advantage is equivalent to a vanishing second moment.
- **claim-density-evolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fnd-recursion)): Let $Q_\ell$ be the marginal likelihood law of the depth-$\ell$ root experiment. Then $$\label{fnd:law-recursion} Q_{\ell+1}=\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}(Q_\ell),\qquad Q_0=\frac13\sum_{i\in\mathcal S}\delta_{3\mathbf e_i},$$ where $\mathbf e_i$ is the coordinate unit vector and, for every bounded measurable $f$, $$\label{fnd:operator} \int f\,d\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}(Q) =\sum_{n\geq0}\mathbb P(D=n) \int Z f(m^{\mathrm{out}})\,dQ^{\otimes n}.$$ In this formula Equation <a href="#fnd:product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:product">[fnd:product]</a> is applied to $m_{i,j}=1+\lambda(\widetilde m_{i,j}-1)$, where $\widetilde m_{\cdot,j}$ are the independent vectors sampled from $Q$. Each conditional integral has total mass one. The operator preserves balanced laws and permutation symmetry.
  - Explanation: The marginal likelihood law at the next depth is obtained from the current one by an explicit operator mixing over the offspring count. The operator preserves balance and permutation symmetry.
- **claim-limiting-posterior** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fnd-limit)): On a common realization of the tree and spins, the root posterior likelihood vectors $m^{(\ell)}$ converge almost surely to a vector $m^{(\infty)}$. Their laws converge weakly to a symmetric balanced law $Q_\infty$ satisfying $$\label{fnd:fixed-point} Q_\infty=\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}(Q_\infty).$$ The advantages $a_\ell$ and moments $\mu_\ell=\mathbb Ex_\ell$ are nonincreasing and converge to their corresponding expectations under $Q_\infty$. This holds throughout $-1/2\leq\lambda\leq1$, including parameters for which edge probabilities vanish.
  - Explanation: Reverse martingale convergence gives almost sure convergence of the root posterior vectors. Their laws converge weakly to a fixed point of the recursion, and the advantage and moments decrease to their limits.
- **claim-log-integrability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fnd-log-integrability)): If $-1/2<\lambda<1$, every fixed point of $\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}$ has strictly positive coordinates almost surely. Every polynomial in its coordinates and their logarithms is integrable under either offspring law in Equation <a href="#fnd:offspring" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:offspring">[fnd:offspring]</a>.
  - Explanation: For interior channel parameters, fixed points have positive coordinates, so polynomials in the coordinates and their logarithms are integrable.
- **claim-extinction-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fnd-survival)): Let $s=1$ in the regular model and let $s$ be the survival probability in the Poisson model. For the limiting experiment, $$\label{fnd:survival-moments} \mu^2\leq s\nu,\qquad \|\eta\|\leq\sqrt{\mu\nu}.$$ In the Poisson model with $d>1$, $0<s<1$ and $$\label{fnd:survival-equation} -\log(1-s)=ds,\qquad s\leq2(1-1/d).$$ In particular, at $d\lambda^2=1$, $s\leq2(1-\lambda^2)$.
  - Explanation: Extinction contributes a uniform posterior, so the limiting moments are controlled by the survival probability, which obeys an explicit equation and bound in the Poisson case.
- **claim-linear-estimator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fnd-supercritical)): In either model, $d\lambda^2>1$ implies reconstruction, for either sign of $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: A normalised order-independent boundary sum has bounded second moment and unit correlation with the root, which proves reconstruction above the threshold.
- **claim-degradation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fnd-degradation)): At every finite depth, each of the following changes produces a degraded root experiment:* 1. *replacing $\lambda$ by $c\lambda$, where $0\leq c\leq1$;* 2. *replacing a regular offspring count $b_+$ by an integer $2\leq b_-\leq b_+$;* 3. *replacing a Poisson offspring mean $d_+$ by $0<d_-\leq d_+$.* *Consequently each change can only decrease the expected total variation advantage and the moments $\mathbb Ex$ and $\mathbb Ex^2$, both at finite depth and in their limits.
  - Explanation: Weakening the channel or reducing the offspring count gives a degraded experiment, so advantage and moments can only decrease.
- **claim-moment-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-moment-bounds)): Put $$c_0=\frac{37}{27},\qquad q=\frac{c_0}{2+\lambda},\qquad c=c_0+2\lambda q.$$ Then $$\label{pos:moment-estimates} 0\le\mathbb EJ\le\mu-\lambda q\nu, \qquad c\nu\le\eta\le\sqrt{\mu\nu}.$$ If $s=1$ in the regular model and $s$ is the survival probability in the Poisson model, then $$\label{pos:moment-region} \frac{\mu^2}{s}\le\nu\le\frac{\mu}{c^2},\qquad \mu\le\frac{s}{c^2}.$$
  - Explanation: At positive equality, an additive entropy-type identity yields explicit bounds linking the limiting moments.
- **claim-correlation-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-correlation-bound)): In either offspring model, $$\label{pos:H-bound} \frac{3H}{\alpha}\ge \frac{3\mu^2}{2(1+2\mu/3)}-\frac\lambda{\sqrt2}\mu\nu.$$
  - Explanation: Summing correlation increments over successive branches bounds the relative entropy from below in terms of the moments.
- **claim-positive-nonreconstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-nonreconstruction)): At every positive equality parameter $d\lambda^2=1$, the limiting posterior is uniform in both tree models. Consequently reconstruction is impossible for $0\le\lambda\le d^{-1/2}$.
  - Explanation: Combining the radial entropy inequality with the moment bounds gives a contradiction at any nonuniform fixed point, so non-reconstruction holds up to positive equality.
- **claim-local-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-gap)): Every nontrivial permutation-invariant fixed point at $da^2=1$ satisfies $\mu>1/20$. This includes $a=1/2$ in both offspring models.
  - Explanation: At negative equality, a nontrivial symmetric fixed point cannot have small second moment; there is a uniform positive gap.
- **claim-pair-inequalities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-pair)): For any current and child posterior vectors, including vectors on the boundary of the simplex, and any $0\le a\le1/2$, $$\begin{aligned} d_n&\le \frac{13}{2}Xz,\qquad -\frac{79}{250}Xz\le d_e\le11Xz,\label{loc:coarsepair}\\ d_x&\le-Xz-2W+2y_0B_0+\frac{51}{20}S,\label{loc:xpair}\\ 6Xz-6W+y_0B_0-\frac{19}{2}S &\le d_e\le6Xz-6W+y_0B_0+S,\label{loc:ypair}\\ C d_n-2d_e&\le-2Xz+22W-7y_0B_0+26S_A+\frac{111}{2}S_B. \label{loc:kpair} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Certified inequalities bound the excess of the invariants when a current message is combined with an edge message, on the whole closed simplex.
- **claim-global-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wei-exclusion)): At $\lambda=-a$, $d=a^{-2}$, the uniform posterior law is the only permutation-invariant fixed point in either of the following ranges:* 1. *regular offspring, with integer $d$ and $0<a\le.448$;* 2. *Poisson offspring, with $0<a\le.477$.* *In particular the regular assertion covers every integer $d\ge5$.
  - Explanation: Away from the coloring channel, the uniform law is the only symmetric fixed point at negative equality in the stated ranges, via a certified weighted-logarithm inequality.
- **claim-weighted-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wei-identities)): Let $0<a<1/2$, $d=a^{-2}$, and let the fixed point be nontrivial. Set $$\label{wei:scalars} \Phi(t)=\mathbb Et^{D},\qquad A=\mathbb EG_e,\qquad z=d(1-A),\qquad h=\frac{1-\Phi(A)}{d(1-A)},\qquad 1-\xi=\frac{\Phi'(A)}d.$$ Then $0<A<1$, $0<\xi<1$, and $$\label{wei:exact} \mathbb ET=h\mathbb E\widehat T,\qquad \mathbb EW=(1-\xi)\mathbb E\widehat W,\qquad \mathbb EY=(1-\xi)\mathbb E\widehat Y,\qquad \mathbb E\widehat T=z.$$
  - Explanation: Exact expectation identities relate the weighted functions of a message to those of its edge versions, for a nontrivial fixed point.
- **claim-scalar-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wei-scalarbounds)): In the regular range of Proposition <a href="#wei:exclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="wei:exclusion">16</a>, or in its Poisson range, one can use constants $$(H,U)=\left(\frac1{16},\frac{15}{32}\right)$$ to obtain $$\label{wei:hz} h\le\overline h:=1-\frac\xi2-H\xi^2-\frac H2\xi^3, \qquad z\ge\underline z:=\xi+U\xi^2.$$ For Poisson offspring the stronger values $(H,U)=(1/12,1/2)$ also hold. Every nontrivial fixed point in the claimed ranges satisfies $.03<\xi<1$.
  - Explanation: Explicit rational constants bound two scalar quantities, and nontrivial fixed points have a scalar parameter bounded away from zero.
- **claim-certified-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wei-positive)): For each row of <a href="#wei:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="wei:parameters">[wei:parameters]</a>, every posterior probability vector, every $a$ in that row’s closed band, and every $.03\le\xi\le1$, the polynomial <a href="#wei:polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="wei:polynomial">[wei:polynomial]</a> satisfies $P>0$.
  - Explanation: An integer interval certificate shows the combined polynomial is strictly positive on the whole certified parameter domain.
- **claim-barycentric-refinement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grid-spread)): Suppose that for every old datum $y$ there are probabilities $\alpha_y(z)$ on a finite set of posterior vectors such that $\sum_z\alpha_y(z)z=p(y)$. Then the posterior law $\nu(z)=\int\alpha_y(z)\rho(dy)$ defines an experiment that dominates the old one.
  - Explanation: Spreading each posterior over finitely many vectors with the same barycentre gives an experiment that is at least as informative.
- **claim-pure-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grid-completion)): Let a finite symmetric posterior law have orbit masses $W_o$, and suppose $0\leq L_o\leq W_o$ for each orbit. The law retaining masses $L_o$ and putting the remaining mass $1-\sum_oL_o$ at the uniformly permuted pure orbit dominates the original experiment.
  - Explanation: Moving rounding and truncation deficits to the pure orbit produces a more informative experiment than the original.
- **claim-negative-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grid-negative-completion)): For both tree models, every admissible negative parameter with $da^2\leq1$ is non-reconstructible (with $d=b$ for a regular tree).
  - Explanation: Finite domination certificates plus the gap and the analytic exclusion cover all negative equalities. Degradation then covers the remaining negative parameters.
- **claim-radial-inequality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rad-lemma)): Let $m=(m_1,m_2,m_3)$ have positive coordinates and $\langle m\rangle=1$, where $\langle w\rangle=\frac13\sum_{i=1}^3w_i$. Put $$v=m-1,\qquad x=\frac12\langle v^2\rangle,\qquad g=\langle \log m\rangle,\qquad u_i=\log m_i-g,$$ $$J=\frac12\langle v\log m\rangle,\qquad B=\frac12\langle m u^2\rangle, \qquad F(m)=3g+4J+\frac45B.$$ Since $I=g+2J$, this is the functional $F$ in Equation <a href="#pos:functional-F" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:functional-F">[pos:functional-F]</a>. Then, for every $0<\lambda<1$, $$\label{rad:inequality} F(m)-\lambda^{-2}F(1+\lambda v) \geq \frac{1-\lambda^2}{2}\,x^2.$$
  - Explanation: A pointwise radial inequality bounds the change of the entropy-type functional under channel contraction from below by a positive multiple of the squared invariant.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fnd-survival",
        "exact_quote": "Let $s=1$ in the regular model and let $s$ be the survival probability in the Poisson model. For the limiting experiment, $$\\label{fnd:survival-moments}\n \\mu^2\\leq s\\nu,\\qquad |\\eta|\\leq\\sqrt{\\mu\\nu}.$$ In the Poisson model with $d>1$, $0<s<1$ and $$\\label{fnd:survival-equation}\n -\\log(1-s)=ds,\\qquad\n s\\leq2(1-1/d).$$ In particular, at $d\\lambda^2=1$, $s\\leq2(1-\\lambda^2)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-229.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fnd-survival",

          "source_id": "PM-229.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-fnd-degradation",

        "anchor": "stmt-pos-moment-bounds",

        "source_id": "PM-229.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-wei-exclusion",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"wei:theorem\" label=\"wei:theorem\"></span>At $\\lambda=-a$, $d=a^{-2}$, the uniform posterior law is the only permutation-invariant fixed point in either of the following ranges:*\n\n1.  *regular offspring, with integer $d$ and $0<a\\le.448$;*\n\n2.  *Poisson offspring, with $0<a\\le.477$.*\n\n*In particular the regular assertion covers every integer $d\\ge5$.",

      "conclusion": {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Symmetric three-state channel and tree models** ([source](#eq:channel)): Let the root spin $\sigma_\rho$ be uniform on $\{1,2,3\}$. Along each edge, independently conditional on its parent spin, transmit through $$\label{eq:channel} K_\lambda(i,j)= \begin{cases} (1+2\lambda)/3,&i=j,\\ (1-\lambda)/3,&i\ne j, \end{cases} \qquad -\tfrac12\le\lambda\le1.$$ The tree is either the rooted $b$-ary tree, with an integer $b\ge2$ children at every vertex, or a Galton–Watson tree with independent $\operatorname{Pois}(d)$ offspring counts, where $d>1$. For the regular model set $d=b$. The tree is independent of the root spin. The observation at depth $\ell$ consists of the unlabelled tree $T_{\le\ell}$ through that depth together with the spins attached to its vertices $\partial T_\ell$ at depth $\ell$; neither interior spins nor an ordering of siblings are observed. Define $$\label{eq:advantage} a_\ell=\mathbb E\left\\| \mathbb P(\sigma_\rho=\cdot\mid T_{\le\ell},\sigma_{\partial T_\ell}) -\operatorname{Unif}\{1,2,3\}\right\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.$$ In the Poisson case this expectation includes both the tree and the spins, *without conditioning on survival*. Reconstruction means $\lim_{\ell\to\infty}a_\ell>0$; non-reconstruction means that this limit is zero. The limit exists by information degradation.
- **Offspring moments** ([source](#fnd:offspring)): We represent the spin set by $\mathcal S=\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}$, where $\omega=\exp(2\pi\mathrm{i}/3)$, and write $S_v$ for the complex spin at a vertex $v$. In the probabilistic recursions, $D$ denotes an offspring count, and $$\label{fnd:offspring} d=\mathbb ED,\qquad \alpha=\frac{\mathbb E[D(D-1)]}{d^2} =\begin{cases}1-1/d,&D\equiv d=b,\\1,&D\sim\operatorname{Pois}(d). \end{cases}$$ All probabilities in the Poisson model are unconditional: the observed tree is part of the data, and extinction is not removed from the sample space.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-ks-threshold | d\lambda^2 | > | $1$ | reconstruction iff strict |
| q-sbm-overlap | limiting expected overlap | = | $\frac13$ | at or below the recovery threshold |
| q-sbm-time | running time of the recovery algorithm | ≤ | $O(n\log n)$ | above the recovery threshold |
| q-local-gap | \mu | > | $1/20$ | da^2=1 |

            "source_id": "PM-229.3"
          },

            "exact_quote": "If $(a-b)^2\\le 3(a+2b)$",

    },

      "effectivity": "effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-sbm",
          "exact_quote": "In the model above, the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *If $(a-b)^2\\le 3(a+2b)$, then every sequence of estimators $\\widehat\\sigma_n$ using only $G_n$ and independent randomization satisfies $$\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}\\mathbb E\\operatorname{ov}(\\sigma,\\widehat\\sigma_n)=\\frac13.$$*\n\n2.  *If $(a-b)^2>3(a+2b)$, there are $\\varepsilon>0$ and an algorithm, allowed to depend on the fixed parameters $a,b$, which runs in $O(n\\log n)$ time and satisfies $$\\mathbb P\\!\\left(\\operatorname{ov}(\\sigma,\\widehat\\sigma_n)\n                  \\ge\\frac13+\\varepsilon\\right)\\longrightarrow1.$$*\n\n*Thus weak recovery is possible exactly under the strict inequality, and is information-theoretically impossible at equality.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-ordering** [claim](#stmt-fnd-ordered) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fnd-ordered): Symmetry of sibling permutations -> Equivalence of ordered and unlabelled observations
- **step-tv-moment** [claim](#stmt-fnd-norms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fnd-norms): Fourier coordinates and Cauchy-Schwarz -> Advantage equivalent to second moment
- **step-recursion** [claim](#stmt-fnd-recursion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fnd-recursion): Edge transmission and conditionally independent branch product -> Density evolution operator
- **step-limit** [claim](#stmt-fnd-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fnd-limit): Reverse martingale convergence and weak continuity -> Fixed-point limit law
- **step-log-integrability** [claim](#stmt-fnd-log-integrability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fnd-log-integrability): Bounds on edge coordinates -> Integrability of logarithmic functionals
- **step-survival** [claim](#stmt-fnd-survival) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fnd-survival): Uniform posterior on extinction and survival equation -> Moment bounds with survival factor
- **step-linear-estimator** [claim](#stmt-fnd-supercritical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fnd-supercritical): Linear boundary estimator with second-moment recursion -> Reconstruction above threshold
- **step-degradation** [claim](#stmt-fnd-degradation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fnd-degradation): Group-increment garbling and thinning -> Monotonicity in channel and offspring
- **step-radial** [claim](#stmt-rad-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rad-lemma): Radial differentiation with polynomial sign certificates -> Radial inequality
- **step-moment-bounds** [claim](#stmt-pos-moment-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-moment-bounds): Integral representations and Jensen -> Moment region
- **step-correlation** [claim](#stmt-pos-correlation-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-correlation-bound): Telescoping correlation increments -> Lower bound on relative entropy
- **step-positive-nonrec** [claim](#stmt-pos-nonreconstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-nonreconstruction): Contradiction from scalar inequalities -> Non-reconstruction at positive equality
- **step-pair** [claim](#stmt-loc-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-pair): Coefficientwise nonnegative polynomials and limits -> Pair inequalities
- **step-gap** [claim](#stmt-loc-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-pair-2): Moment bootstrap with pair inequalities -> Uniform second-moment gap
- **step-weighted-identities** [claim](#stmt-wei-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wei-identities): Additivity of centered logarithms -> Exact weighted identities
- **step-scalar-bounds** [claim](#stmt-wei-scalarbounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wei-scalarbounds): Binomial series truncation -> Scalar bounds
- **step-certified-positivity** [claim](#stmt-wei-positive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wei-positive): Integer interval arithmetic with Bernstein coefficients -> Strict positivity of the polynomial
- **step-global-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-wei-exclusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wei-positive-2): Zero-mean expression versus pointwise positivity -> Exclusion of nontrivial fixed points
- **step-spread** [claim](#stmt-grid-spread) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-spread): Explicit refinement kernel -> Dominating barycentric experiment
- **step-completion** [claim](#stmt-grid-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-completion): Revealing the spin with orbit-constant probability -> Dominating completed law
- **step-negative-completion** [claim](#stmt-grid-negative-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-negative-completion): Finite domination certificate, gap, analytic exclusion and degradation -> Non-reconstruction at all negative parameters up to equality
- **step-threshold** [claim](#stmt-thm-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-negative-completion-2): Assembly of supercritical and equality cases -> Exact threshold
- **step-sbm** [claim](#stmt-cor-sbm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-negative-completion-3): Tree-to-graph transfer and external algorithm -> Weak-recovery threshold

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-spread-mean**: The grid spread of a nonnegative integer vector r with positive total T returns grid vectors with nonnegative integer coordinates summing to H, with probabilities summing to one, whose mean is r/T.
- **inv-spread-cases**: The spread has at most one, at most three, or at most three support vectors according to whether the deficit k is zero, one or two. Their probabilities are T over T, b_i over T, or (T minus b_i) over T.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-orbit-index-only**: Keep only the orbit index of the spread vector instead of the full labelled grid vector. -> The retained datum is a different, weaker experiment, so the domination argument no longer applies.
- **anti-zero-total**: Apply the spreading rule to the zero vector. -> Division by the total T is undefined, so no probabilities are produced.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The barycentric grid spreading rule is an explicit finite integer construction: floors, remainders and a deficit decide a short list of grid vectors with integer weights, which can be checked exactly.

```python
# INPUTS: one line with integers H r0 r1 r2 (grid size and a nonnegative integer vector)
# PRECONDITIONS: H >= 1, all r_i >= 0, T = r0+r1+r2 > 0
# OUTPUTS: one line per support vector: h0 h1 h2 w, meaning probability w/T for grid vector h/H
# TIME: constant (three coordinates)
# SPACE: constant
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers, floor division only
# FAILURE: inputs violating the preconditions are not handled
import sys

def main():
    H, r0, r1, r2 = map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())
    r = [r0, r1, r2]
    T = sum(r)
    n = [H*x//T for x in r]
    b = [H*r[i]-n[i]*T for i in range(3)]
    k = H-sum(n)
    out = []
    if k == 0:
        out.append((n, T))
    elif k == 1:
        for i in range(3):
            if b[i] > 0:
                v = list(n)
                v[i] += 1
                out.append((v, b[i]))
    else:
        for i in range(3):
            if T-b[i] > 0:
                v = [x+1 for x in n]
                v[i] -= 1
                out.append((v, T-b[i]))
    for v, w in out:
        print(v[0], v[1], v[2], w)

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fast recovery procedure for the three-community model is only cited from earlier work, and the paper prints no algorithm for it. The only code it ships is a set of proof-checking programs, whose source is not included in the text, so there is nothing here to implement as a library. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper deliberately keeps sampling and simulation out of its argument: every certification step is exact arithmetic. Random label generation appears only as the definition of the model, not as a simulation recipe. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The verification programs are described as part of the proof, but their sources are not reproduced in the text, so no reusable tool contract can be extracted. Their meaning is argued in prose only. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite-experiment section gives an explicit integer procedure: combine two grid mass laws through a precomputed table, round every orbit mass down, and give the lost mass to the pure orbit, so the result dominates the exact product. Iterating this and testing the integer second-moment condition is a concrete certificate recursion. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The permutation-invariant posterior law is stored compactly as one mass per orbit of sorted grid triples, a fixed-size integer vector plus lookup tables for pair products. This representation is reusable for symmetric discrete distributions with a small symmetry group. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Wide integer arithmetic is used only to keep the proof checks overflow-free on an ordinary machine. Nothing in the results concerns accelerator design or hardware performance. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Impossibility of recovering community names is a statement about information in the graph, not about computational hardness or secrecy. No adversary, key or primitive appears. |
| machine_learning | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | For unsupervised three-way clustering of a sparse graph, the corollary gives a sharp feasibility test: weak recovery is possible exactly under a strict inequality in the within- and between-community rates, and impossible at equality or below. Use it as a go/no-go check before running any clustering method. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a sharp threshold for statistical inference. It contains no objective function, constraint set or optimisation procedure whose optimum is characterised. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The broadcast process is an analysis model with noise independent across edges and a tree independent of the root value. It does not describe a protocol, failure model or network assumptions of a real distributed system. |
| other (statistical inference thresholds on trees) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The main theorem gives an exact decision rule for whether a root symbol can be inferred from deep noisy descendants on a regular or Poisson tree: compare the branching parameter times the squared channel eigenvalue with one, strictly. This is a usable feasibility predicate for statistical-physics style inference questions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-poisson-unconditional** (hypothesis): The Poisson advantage is averaged over trees and spins without conditioning on survival. The threshold is not a statement about the surviving tree alone. Blocked use: ['Quoting the threshold for a tree already known to survive', 'Comparing it with results that condition on survival']
- **limit-sbm-positive-rates** (hypothesis): The block-model corollary needs strictly positive within- and between-community rates and mean degree above one. The boundary cases with a zero rate are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the weak-recovery test when a rate is zero', 'Applying it at mean degree at most one']
- **limit-three-state-symmetric** (formal_scope): The model has a uniform root spin on three states and one symmetric channel on every edge. Nothing is claimed for other numbers of states or asymmetric channels. Blocked use: ['Transferring the exact threshold to channels with more states', 'Using it for edge-dependent or asymmetric channels']
- **limit-equality-nonreconstruction** (edge_case): At the critical equality there is non-reconstruction, for either sign of the channel parameter. Equality is therefore not on the reconstructing side. Blocked use: ['Designing leaf-to-root inference expecting a nonzero advantage at equality', 'Treating the weak-recovery test as non-strict']
- **limit-negative-equality-range** (hypothesis): The negative-equality analysis assumes a negative channel parameter with branching parameter at least four. Smaller branching is handled only by degradation from the coloring case. Blocked use: ['Applying the local gap to branching parameter below four', 'Applying the gap at a positive channel parameter']
- **limit-certificate-execution** (formal_scope): The finite certificates are computer-assisted and count as proof only when run with assertions enabled on the stated sources. A build with assertions disabled is not the certificate. Blocked use: ['Accepting a certificate run compiled without assertions', 'Reusing the certificate for parameter bins outside those covered']
- **limit-grid-stopping-test** (approximation): The finite-experiment recursion certifies non-reconstruction only through a strict integer stopping test on the discretised second moment. The upper experiment need not converge to the uniform one. Blocked use: ['Reading the stopping depth as a convergence rate of the true process', 'Using a non-strict or floating-point stopping comparison']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 229 (`lean/docs/229.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The exact reconstruction threshold for the three-state symmetric channel](../../preprints/The-exact-reconstruction-threshold-for-the-three-state-symmetric-channel-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the supercritical direction of reconstruction for the symmetric three-state broadcast channel, whose parameter satisfies $-1/2\le\lambda\le1$. On a regular $b$-ary tree, reconstruction holds when $b\lambda^2>1$; on an observed Poisson Galton–Watson tree of mean $d$, it holds when $d\lambda^2>1$. In each case the root-estimation advantage converges to a positive limit, with the Poisson advantage averaged over trees and spins.
>
> The selected statements cover reconstruction above the Kesten–Stigum threshold. Non-reconstruction at or below the threshold and the stochastic-block-model consequences in the paper are outside them.

Comparator source `ThreeStateTreeClauses.lean` (3794 bytes, sha256 `70eabccad9babe51e425cbdceef6be93c00b12771ea6eb6102296c15a64353f5`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators ENNReal NNReal
open Filter

namespace ThreeState.TreeClauses

abbrev Spin := Fin 3

def Admissible (lam : ℝ) : Prop := -(1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ lam ∧ lam ≤ 1

def channelWeight (lam : ℝ) (i j : Spin) : ℝ :=
  if i = j then (1 + 2 * lam) / 3 else (1 - lam) / 3

lemma channelWeight_nonneg {lam : ℝ} (hlam : Admissible lam) (i j : Spin) :
    0 ≤ channelWeight lam i j := by
  unfold channelWeight
  split_ifs <;> dsimp [Admissible] at hlam <;> linarith [hlam.1, hlam.2]

lemma channelWeight_sum (lam : ℝ) (i : Spin) : ∑ j, channelWeight lam i j = 1 := by
  fin_cases i <;> simp [channelWeight, Fin.sum_univ_three] <;> ring

def channel (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam) (i : Spin) : PMF Spin :=
  PMF.ofFintype (fun j ↦ ENNReal.ofReal (channelWeight lam i j)) (by
    rw [← ENNReal.ofReal_sum_of_nonneg (fun j _ ↦ channelWeight_nonneg hlam i j),
      channelWeight_sum]
    norm_num)

def Observation : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Spin
  | n + 1 => Multiset (Observation n)

def iidList {α : Type} (p : PMF α) : ℕ → PMF (List α)
  | 0 => PMF.pure []
  | n + 1 => p.bind fun x ↦ (iidList p n).map (List.cons x)

def observationLaw (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam) (offspring : PMF ℕ) :
    (ℓ : ℕ) → Spin → PMF (Observation ℓ)
  | 0, i => PMF.pure i
  | ℓ + 1, i => offspring.bind fun n ↦
      (iidList ((channel lam hlam i).bind (observationLaw lam hlam offspring ℓ)) n).map
        (fun xs ↦ (xs : Multiset (Observation ℓ)))

def jointWeight (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam) (offspring : PMF ℕ)
    (ℓ : ℕ) (i : Spin) (o : Observation ℓ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  (1 / 3 : ℝ≥0∞) * observationLaw lam hlam offspring ℓ i o

def marginalWeight (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam) (offspring : PMF ℕ)
    (ℓ : ℕ) (o : Observation ℓ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ∑ i, jointWeight lam hlam offspring ℓ i o

def posterior (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam) (offspring : PMF ℕ)
    (ℓ : ℕ) (o : Observation ℓ) (i : Spin) : ℝ :=
  (jointWeight lam hlam offspring ℓ i o / marginalWeight lam hlam offspring ℓ o).toReal

def advantage (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam) (offspring : PMF ℕ) (ℓ : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  ∑' o : Observation ℓ, (marginalWeight lam hlam offspring ℓ o).toReal *
    ((∑ i : Spin, |posterior lam hlam offspring ℓ o i - (1 / 3 : ℝ)|) / 2)

def Reconstructs (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam) (offspring : PMF ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ a : ℝ, 0 < a ∧ Tendsto (advantage lam hlam offspring) atTop (nhds a)

namespace Probability

def poissonWeight (rate : ℝ) (count : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  Real.exp (-rate) * rate ^ count / (count.factorial : ℝ)

lemma poissonWeight_nonneg {rate : ℝ} (hrate : 0 ≤ rate) (count : ℕ) :
    0 ≤ poissonWeight rate count := by
  unfold poissonWeight
  positivity

lemma poissonWeight_mass (rate : ℝ≥0) : HasSum (poissonWeight rate) 1 :=
  ProbabilityTheory.hasSum_one_poissonMeasure rate

def _root_.OAI.ThreeState.TreeClauses.poissonPMF (rate : ℝ≥0) : PMF ℕ := by
  refine ⟨fun count ↦ ENNReal.ofReal (poissonWeight rate count), ?_⟩
  apply ENNReal.hasSum_coe.mpr
  rw [← Real.toNNReal_one]
  exact (poissonWeight_mass rate).toNNReal (poissonWeight_nonneg rate.coe_nonneg)

end Probability

end ThreeState.TreeClauses

open scoped Topology
namespace ThreeState.TreeClauses.Tree

theorem regular_reconstructs_above_KS (b : ℕ) (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam)
    (hKS : 1 < (b:ℝ)*lam^2) : Reconstructs lam hlam (PMF.pure b) := by
  sorry

theorem poisson_reconstructs_above_KS (d : ℝ≥0) (lam : ℝ) (hlam : Admissible lam)
    (hKS : 1 < (d:ℝ)*lam^2) : Reconstructs lam hlam (poissonPMF d) := by
  sorry

end ThreeState.TreeClauses.Tree

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We determine the exact reconstruction threshold for the symmetric three-state broadcast process on every regular <i>b</i>-ary tree, <i>b</i> ≥ 2, and every observed Poisson Galton–Watson tree of mean <i>d</i> &gt; 1. Reconstruction occurs exactly when $`d\lambda^2\gt 1`$, with <i>d</i> = <i>b</i> in the regular model; there is non-reconstruction at equality for either sign of the channel parameter. The Poisson advantage is averaged over trees and spins without conditioning on survival. This resolves the all-degree three-state regular-tree prediction. Combining the Poisson theorem with known tree-to-graph and algorithmic results gives the exact weak-recovery threshold for the symmetric three-community sparse stochastic block model with independent uniform labels, fixed within- and between-community rates $`a,b\gt 0`$, and mean degree $`(a+2b)/3\gt 1`$: recovery is possible exactly when $`(a-b)^2\gt 3(a+2b)`$. Above this threshold it is achievable in $`O(n\log n)`$ time; at or below it, weak recovery is information-theoretically impossible.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The reconstruction problem asks whether many distant, noisy descendants retain information about their common ancestor. On a tree, the growth in the number of descendants competes with the loss of information across each edge. For a symmetric channel with nontrivial eigenvalue $\lambda$ and mean offspring number $d$, the Kesten–Stigum condition $d\lambda^2>1$ gives reconstruction. Whether this condition is also necessary depends on the channel. We determine the answer for three states at every branching parameter in two basic tree families. Through existing tree-to-graph and algorithmic results, the Poisson theorem also determines the weak-recovery threshold for the symmetric three-community sparse stochastic block model with positive connectivity parameters and mean degree greater than one.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Model and result

<a id="eq:channel"></a>
Let the root spin $\sigma_\rho$ be uniform on $\{1,2,3\}$. Along each edge, independently conditional on its parent spin, transmit through $$\label{eq:channel}
 K_\lambda(i,j)=
 \begin{cases}
 (1+2\lambda)/3,&i=j,\\
 (1-\lambda)/3,&i\ne j,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad -\tfrac12\le\lambda\le1.$$ The tree is either the rooted $b$-ary tree, with an integer $b\ge2$ children at every vertex, or a Galton–Watson tree with independent $\operatorname{Pois}(d)$ offspring counts, where $d>1$. For the regular model set $d=b$. The tree is independent of the root spin. The observation at depth $\ell$ consists of the unlabelled tree $T_{\le\ell}$ through that depth together with the spins attached to its vertices $\partial T_\ell$ at depth $\ell$; neither interior spins nor an ordering of siblings are observed. Define <a id="eq:advantage"></a>$$\label{eq:advantage}
 a_\ell=\mathbb E\left\|
 \mathbb P(\sigma_\rho=\cdot\mid T_{\le\ell},\sigma_{\partial T_\ell})
 -\operatorname{Unif}\{1,2,3\}\right\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.$$ In the Poisson case this expectation includes both the tree and the spins, *without conditioning on survival*. Reconstruction means $\lim_{\ell\to\infty}a_\ell>0$; non-reconstruction means that this limit is zero. The limit exists by information degradation.

<a id="stmt-thm-threshold"></a>
<div id="thm:threshold" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Exact threshold). *For every integer $b\ge2$ in the regular model and every real $d>1$ in the Poisson model, reconstruction occurs if and only if $$d\lambda^2>1,$$ with $d=b$ in the regular case. At every admissible equality $d\lambda^2=1$, there is non-reconstruction.*

</div>

Thus the positive threshold is $\lambda=d^{-1/2}$. On the negative side reconstruction occurs precisely when $d>4$ and $-1/2\le\lambda<-d^{-1/2}$. In particular, the three-coloring channel $\lambda=-1/2$ is non-reconstructible at branching parameter four in both models.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### History and significance

The branching-process work of Kesten and Stigum (Kesten and Stigum 1966) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[KestenStigum1966](#ref-KestenStigum1966)] underlies the classical second-moment reconstruction bound. Broadcasting and its relation to the Ising model were developed by Evans, Kenyon, Peres, and Schulman (Evans et al. 2000) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[EvansEtAl2000](#ref-EvansEtAl2000)]. For more than two states, nonlinear information in the boundary can change the threshold. This possibility occurs for symmetric channels with five or more states (Sly 2011, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Sly2011](#ref-Sly2011)]; the three-state assertion therefore needs more than the second-moment bound.

Mézard and Montanari (Mézard and Montanari 2006, Conjecture 2 and the following paragraph) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[MezardMontanari2006](#ref-MezardMontanari2006)] predicted sharpness of the Kesten–Stigum bound for three-state symmetric channels of either sign up to a branching cutoff, and proposed the extension to all branching parameters. See also Conjecture 1 of Sly (Sly 2011) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Sly2011](#ref-Sly2011)] for the all-degree regular-tree formulation. Sly proved this for all sufficiently large regular branching parameters, including non-reconstruction at equality (Sly 2011, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Sly2011](#ref-Sly2011)]. Mossel, Sly, and Sohn established the corresponding large-mean Poisson-tree result, for both signs and including equality (Mossel, Sly, and Sohn 2025, Theorem 1.12) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[MosselSlySohn2025](#ref-MosselSlySohn2025)]. At small degrees, Bhatnagar and Maneva developed finite representations of posterior laws and proved, among other bounds, non-reconstruction for three-colorings on the regular ternary tree (Bhatnagar and Maneva 2011, Theorem 12) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BhatnagarManeva2011](#ref-BhatnagarManeva2011)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:threshold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:threshold">1</a> removes the large-branching restriction in these two tree families. It resolves the three-state regular-tree prediction in its all-degree form and includes the low-branching and coloring endpoints for the observed Poisson tree.

For the symmetric stochastic block model, the cavity-method analysis of Decelle, Krzakala, Moore, and Zdeborová predicted the Kesten–Stigum transition in the assortative three-community case (Decelle et al. 2011, sec. IV.A) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DecelleEtAl2011](#ref-DecelleEtAl2011)]. Ricci-Tersenghi, Semerjian, and Zdeborová gave further evidence for the three-state prediction for either sign, through local analysis of the posterior recursion and numerical cavity solutions (Ricci-Tersenghi, Semerjian, and Zdeborová 2019, sec. III.D.1 and IV.H) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[RicciTersenghiEtAl2019](#ref-RicciTersenghiEtAl2019)]. Mossel, Sly, and Sohn proved information-theoretic impossibility at and below the Kesten–Stigum bound for three or four communities when the mean degree is sufficiently large (Mossel, Sly, and Sohn 2025, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[MosselSlySohn2025](#ref-MosselSlySohn2025)]. Their tree-to-graph transfer has no large-degree restriction (Mossel, Sly, and Sohn 2025, Theorem 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[MosselSlySohn2025](#ref-MosselSlySohn2025)]. Together with the above-threshold algorithm of Abbe and Sandon (Abbe and Sandon 2018, Corollary 1 and Lemma 1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[AbbeSandon2018](#ref-AbbeSandon2018)], the Poisson part of our theorem therefore gives the exact three-community weak-recovery threshold stated in Corollary <a href="#cor:sbm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:sbm">2</a> below.

A related three-state model with branch-dependent couplings on a binary Cayley tree is studied by Mukhamedov and Akın (Mukhamedov and Akın 2026) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MukhamedovAkin2026](#ref-MukhamedovAkin2026)]. Their work concerns disordered translation-invariant Gibbs measures and their extremality; our theorem concerns the same homogeneous symmetric channel on every edge of the regular or observed Poisson tree.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy and its precedents

For a root posterior $p=(p_1,p_2,p_3)$, write $m_i=3p_i$ and identify the three spin labels with $1,\omega,\omega^2$, where $\omega=e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}/3}$. The complex posterior mean and its two basic invariants are $$\zeta=p_1+\omega p_2+\omega^2p_3,\qquad
 x=|\zeta|^2,\qquad y=\Re(\zeta^3).$$ The posterior recursion passes each child experiment through one edge and combines the resulting conditionally independent branch observations. Section <a href="#fnd:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:section">2</a> gives this recursion and proves that the limiting posterior law is a fixed point, including at the coloring endpoint. Non-reconstruction is equivalent to $\mathbb Ex=0$ under that law. A linear estimator proves reconstruction above the threshold. Channel and offspring degradation reduce the converse to critical equality and the coloring case at branching parameter four.

At positive equality, Section <a href="#sec:positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:positive">3</a> compares two expressions for the change of an entropy functional across the recursion. The expected symmetrized-entropy identity goes back to Formentin and Külske (Formentin and Külske 2009, Proposition 3.3 and equation (15)) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[FormentinKulske2009Entropy](#ref-FormentinKulske2009Entropy)]. Our functional combines entropy and symmetrized entropy with a quadratic centered-logarithm term. The fixed-point identities express its change through the correlation generated by combining branches, while a radial inequality bounds it below by a positive multiple of $\mathbb Ex^2$. Moment estimates make these two bounds incompatible at a nonuniform limiting fixed point. Appendix <a href="#rad:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rad:section">8</a> proves the radial inequality analytically. Related entropy methods include the sharp nonlinear information-contraction inequalities for the Potts channel and non-reconstruction criteria of Gu and Polyanskiy (Gu and Polyanskiy 2023) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[GuPolyanskiy2023](#ref-GuPolyanskiy2023)].

The negative argument separates small and large posterior moments. Sly’s large-branching proof already combines a moment estimate near the uniform posterior with a global Gaussian contraction, including a rigorous numerical verification (Sly 2011, sec. 1.5, Lemmas 4.7–4.8, and Appendix A) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Sly2011](#ref-Sly2011)]. Here Section <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">4</a> proves a uniform quantitative gap: at every negative equality, any nonuniform symmetric fixed point satisfies $\mathbb Ex>1/20$. Pair inequalities for $x$, $y$, and $x^2$ yield this gap through a moment bootstrap. Section <a href="#sec:weighted" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:weighted">5</a> then uses weighted logarithm identities to exclude the remaining fixed points away from the coloring channel.

Near coloring, Section <a href="#grid:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="grid:section">6</a> constructs finite experiments that are more informative than the true boundary observation. It suffices for one such upper experiment to have $\mathbb Ex<1/20$: the true limiting fixed point then falls below the gap and must be uniform. The upper recursion need not itself converge to the uniform experiment. Bhatnagar and Maneva introduced finite surveys of posterior laws and combined certified finite recursion with local contraction (Bhatnagar and Maneva 2011, secs. 3.1–3.3 and Theorem 12) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[BhatnagarManeva2011](#ref-BhatnagarManeva2011)]. Their construction includes completion by perfectly informative messages for omitted offspring tails and downward-rounded masses (Bhatnagar and Maneva 2011, sec. 3.3, implementation remarks 1 and 4) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[BhatnagarManeva2011](#ref-BhatnagarManeva2011)]. We use this finite-experiment approach with the gap above and explicit three-state refinement kernels. Gu’s recent binary-hypertree work proves a related rigorous population-dynamics procedure and proposes a Potts extension based on degradation and local non-reconstruction (Gu 2026, sec. 4 and 7) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Gu2026Hypertrees](#ref-Gu2026Hypertrees)].

The quantitative radial bound, uniform fixed-point gap, and exact certificates supply the estimates needed throughout the parameter range. The negative argument is computer-assisted: the polynomial, interval, and finite-experiment programs are printed in Appendix <a href="#app:certificates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:certificates">11</a>. Their mathematical interpretation and arithmetic bounds are proved in the text. All decimal constants denote exact terminating rationals, and no simulation estimate enters a certification decision. Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">7</a> assembles the tree classification; Table <a href="#tab:coverage" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:coverage">1</a> there records the parameter ranges of its analytic and finite arguments.

<a id="sec:sbm"></a>
#### A consequence for community detection

We now use $a$ and $b$ for within- and between-community connectivity parameters, independently of the notation for regular branching above. Fix $a,b>0$ with $d=(a+2b)/3>1$. For all sufficiently large $n$, choose $\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_n$ independently and uniformly from $\{1,2,3\}$. Conditional on these labels, form a graph $G_n$ on $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ by including each unordered pair independently, with probability $a/n$ if its endpoints have the same label and $b/n$ otherwise. For two labelings $\sigma,\widehat\sigma\in\{1,2,3\}^n$, define their overlap by $$\operatorname{ov}(\sigma,\widehat\sigma)
 =\max_{\pi\in S_3}\frac1n\sum_{v=1}^n
   \mathbf1_{\{\widehat\sigma_v=\pi(\sigma_v)\}}.$$ The permutation accounts for the fact that the names of the communities cannot be recovered from the graph.

<a id="stmt-cor-sbm"></a>
<div id="cor:sbm" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Exact weak-recovery threshold for three communities). *In the model above, the following statements hold.*

1.  *If $(a-b)^2\le 3(a+2b)$, then every sequence of estimators $\widehat\sigma_n$ using only $G_n$ and independent randomization satisfies $$\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb E\operatorname{ov}(\sigma,\widehat\sigma_n)=\frac13.$$*

2.  *If $(a-b)^2>3(a+2b)$, there are $\varepsilon>0$ and an algorithm, allowed to depend on the fixed parameters $a,b$, which runs in $O(n\log n)$ time and satisfies $$\mathbb P\!\left(\operatorname{ov}(\sigma,\widehat\sigma_n)
                  \ge\frac13+\varepsilon\right)\longrightarrow1.$$*

*Thus weak recovery is possible exactly under the strict inequality, and is information-theoretically impossible at equality.*

</div>

The proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:sbm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:sbm">2</a>, including the passage from averaged tree non-reconstruction to the fixed-tree hypothesis of the transfer theorem, is given in Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">7</a>.

<a id="fnd:section"></a>
### Posterior experiments and reductions

<a id="fnd:offspring"></a>
We represent the spin set by $\mathcal S=\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}$, where $\omega=\exp(2\pi\mathrm{i}/3)$, and write $S_v$ for the complex spin at a vertex $v$. In the probabilistic recursions, $D$ denotes an offspring count, and $$\label{fnd:offspring}
 d=\mathbb ED,\qquad
 \alpha=\frac{\mathbb E[D(D-1)]}{d^2}
 =\begin{cases}1-1/d,&D\equiv d=b,\\1,&D\sim\operatorname{Pois}(d).
 \end{cases}$$ All probabilities in the Poisson model are unconditional: the observed tree is part of the data, and extinction is not removed from the sample space.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Observations, likelihoods, and posterior coordinates

For calculations we construct the tree with ordered children. At each vertex, independently, we draw its offspring count and then the spins of its children through $K_\lambda$. The root spin is uniform and is independent of all offspring counts. The resulting tree is locally finite almost surely. Let $$O_\ell=(T_{\leq\ell},(S_v)_{|v|=\ell})$$ denote its depth-$\ell$ observation, initially with this auxiliary ordering retained. The unlabelled observation retains the rooted tree and its boundary-spin decoration, but forgets all sibling orders.

<a id="stmt-fnd-ordered"></a>
<div id="fnd:ordered" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Removing sibling orders). *The ordered and unlabelled depth observations are equivalent for inference of the root spin. In particular, they have the same reconstruction advantage and the same distribution of the root posterior.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fnd-ordered"><summary>Proof of stmt-fnd-ordered</summary>

*Proof.* Fix a finite unlabelled boundary-decorated tree. Conditional on any specified root spin, the probabilities of all its distinct ordered representatives are equal. Indeed, a permutation of siblings permutes independent, identically distributed descendant constructions; summing over unobserved internal spins preserves this invariance. The group of such sibling permutations is transitive on the distinct representatives. Repeated representatives caused by identical subtrees have equal stabilizer multiplicities, so they do not alter the equality of these probabilities.

Consequently, conditional on the unlabelled observation, the law of the ordering is independent of the root spin. Forgetting the ordering is a deterministic operation, and sampling an ordering from this conditional law is an operation that does not use the root spin. These two operations establish the claimed equivalence, including when the boundary is empty. ◻

</details>

<a id="fnd:fourier"></a>
<a id="fnd:cubic"></a>
<a id="fnd:invariant-bounds"></a>
<a id="fnd:ratio-domain"></a>
An *experiment* consists of a uniform spin $S\in\mathcal S$ and an observed datum $Y$. Write <a id="fnd:posterior"></a>$$\label{fnd:posterior}
 p_i=\mathbb P(S=i\mid Y),\qquad m_i=3p_i,\qquad v_i=m_i-1,
 \qquad i\in\mathcal S.$$ Thus the likelihood vector $m$ takes values in the compact simplex $$\mathcal M=\{(m_i)_{i\in\mathcal S}:m_i\geq0,\ \textstyle\sum_i m_i=3\}.$$ For coordinatewise expressions we use $\langle h\rangle=\frac13\sum_{i\in\mathcal S}h_i$. When coordinates are written as numerical triples indexed by $0,1,2$, coordinate $i\in\{0,1,2\}$ corresponds to the spin label $\omega^i$. The complex posterior and its two basic invariants are <a id="fnd:complex"></a>$$\label{fnd:complex}
 \zeta=\mathbb E[S\mid Y]=\langle i m_i\rangle,\qquad
 x=|\zeta|^2,\qquad y=\operatorname{Re}(\zeta^3).$$ Here the $i$ inside the coordinate average is the spin label, not the imaginary unit. Fourier inversion on the three-element group gives $$\label{fnd:fourier}
 v_i=\zeta\overline{i}+\overline{\zeta}i
     =2\operatorname{Re}(\zeta\overline{i}),\qquad
 \langle v^2\rangle=2x,\qquad \langle v^3\rangle=2y.$$ More precisely, each of the three coordinates satisfies $$\label{fnd:cubic}
 v_i^3=3xv_i+2y,\qquad
 \prod_{i\in\mathcal S}(1+t v_i)=1-3t^2x+2t^3y.$$ To verify the first identity, expand $(\zeta\overline{i}+\overline{\zeta}i)^3$ and use $i^3=1$. The second follows from $\sum_i v_i=0$, $\sum_i v_i^2=6x$, and $\prod_i v_i=2y$. The convex-hull constraint on $\zeta$, together with $\prod_i m_i\geq0$, gives $$\label{fnd:invariant-bounds}
 0\leq x\leq1,\qquad |y|\leq x^{3/2},\qquad 1-3x+2y\geq0.$$ For later use these also imply, when $x>0$, $$\label{fnd:ratio-domain}
 -\frac12\leq r:=\frac yx\leq1,\qquad
 r^2\leq x\leq\frac1{3-2r}.$$ Indeed $|r|\leq\sqrt{x}\leq1$, and the last inequality in Equation <a href="#fnd:invariant-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:invariant-bounds">[fnd:invariant-bounds]</a> gives the upper bound on $x$. Combining the two bounds on $x$ gives $0\leq1-3r^2+2r^3=(1-r)^2(1+2r)$, and hence $r\geq-1/2$.

The marginal law of a posterior likelihood vector is always balanced: $\mathbb Em_i=1$. Bayes’ formula states that its conditional law under input $i$ is tilted by $m_i$. More generally, for a bounded measurable function $g$ of the original datum, <a id="fnd:bayes-tilt"></a>$$\label{fnd:bayes-tilt}
 \mathbb E[g(Y)\mid S=i]=\mathbb E[m_i(Y)g(Y)].$$ Conversely, every probability law $Q$ on $\mathcal M$ satisfying $\int m_i\,Q(dm)=1$ defines an experiment by taking $Q(dm\mid S=i)=m_iQ(dm)$. Its posterior likelihood vector is precisely the observed $m$. Thus balanced laws on $\mathcal M$ are sufficient to describe the experiments used below.

The posterior vector also loses no inferential information from an existing experiment. In fact $\mathbb P(S=i\mid Y,m)=m_i/3=\mathbb P(S=i\mid m)$, so $S$ and $Y$ are conditionally independent given $m$. The original datum can therefore be conditionally simulated from $m$ without knowing $S$. All observation spaces here are standard Borel, so the required conditional kernels exist.

Our experiments are invariant under every permutation of the three spin labels. In complex coordinates these permutations are generated by $\zeta\mapsto\omega\zeta$ and $\zeta\mapsto\overline{\zeta}$. This invariance is retained by all the tree recursions and limits below.

<a id="stmt-fnd-norms"></a>
<div id="fnd:norms" class="lemma">

<a id="fnd:tv-pointwise"></a>
<a id="fnd:tv-moment"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Total variation and the posterior second moment). *For every posterior vector, $$\label{fnd:tv-pointwise}
 \frac{|\zeta|}{\sqrt3}
 \leq\left\|p-\operatorname{Unif}(\mathcal S)\right\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
 \leq\frac23|\zeta|.$$ Consequently, writing $\mu_\ell=\mathbb Ex_\ell$ for the depth experiment and $a_\ell$ for its mean total variation advantage, $$\label{fnd:tv-moment}
 \frac{\mu_\ell}{\sqrt3}\leq a_\ell
 \leq\frac23\sqrt{\mu_\ell}.$$ In particular, vanishing advantage is equivalent to vanishing posterior second moment.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fnd-norms"><summary>Proof of stmt-fnd-norms</summary>

*Proof.* For any three real numbers with zero sum, half the sum of their absolute values equals their largest absolute value. Applying this to $v$ gives $\|p-\operatorname{Unif}(\mathcal S)\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
=\max_i|v_i|/3$. If $M=\max_i|v_i|$, their squared sum is at most $2M^2$: after choosing the coordinate of absolute value $M$, the other two have the opposite sign and their absolute values sum to $M$. Equation <a href="#fnd:fourier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:fourier">[fnd:fourier]</a> therefore yields $6x\leq2M^2$. The same equation gives $M\leq2|\zeta|$, proving Equation <a href="#fnd:tv-pointwise" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:tv-pointwise">[fnd:tv-pointwise]</a>. Now use $|\zeta|^2\leq|\zeta|$ and Cauchy–Schwarz to obtain Equation <a href="#fnd:tv-moment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:tv-moment">[fnd:tv-moment]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The exact posterior recursion

<a id="fnd:edge"></a>
Suppose $Y$ is a child experiment with likelihood vector $m$. The child spin has a uniform marginal, and the conditional law of $Y$ given parent spin $i$, relative to the child marginal law, has density $$\label{fnd:edge}
 m_{e,i}=\sum_{k\in\mathcal S}K_\lambda(i,k)m_k
        =1+\lambda(m_i-1)=1+\lambda v_i.$$ Thus the datum retains its original marginal law under the uniform parent prior. The edge operation sends <a id="fnd:edge-moments"></a>$$\label{fnd:edge-moments}
 \zeta\longmapsto\lambda\zeta,\qquad
 x\longmapsto\lambda^2x,\qquad y\longmapsto\lambda^3y.$$

<a id="fnd:product"></a>
To combine $n$ experiments that are independent conditional on a common input, let $Q_1,\ldots,Q_n$ be their marginal likelihood laws. Under the *product marginal law* $Q_1\otimes\cdots\otimes Q_n$, set $$\label{fnd:product}
 W_i=\prod_{j=1}^n m_{i,j},\qquad
 Z=\langle W\rangle=\frac13\sum_{i\in\mathcal S}W_i,\qquad
 m_i^{\mathrm{out}}=\frac{W_i}{Z}\quad(Z>0).$$ Conditional on input $i$, the joint datum has density $W_i$ relative to that product law. Averaging over the input gives the marginal density $Z$, and Bayes’ formula gives the last expression in Equation <a href="#fnd:product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:product">[fnd:product]</a>. In particular, $\int Z\,d(Q_1\otimes\cdots\otimes Q_n)=1$. The value of $m^{\mathrm{out}}$ on $\{Z=0\}$ may be set equal to $(1,1,1)$; this set has zero probability after the tilt. When $n=0$, empty products are one, $Z=1$, and the output is the uninformative vector $(1,1,1)$.

<a id="stmt-fnd-recursion"></a>
<div id="fnd:recursion" class="proposition">

<a id="fnd:law-recursion"></a>
<a id="fnd:operator"></a>
**Proposition 5** (Density evolution). *Let $Q_\ell$ be the marginal likelihood law of the depth-$\ell$ root experiment. Then $$\label{fnd:law-recursion}
 Q_{\ell+1}=\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}(Q_\ell),\qquad
 Q_0=\frac13\sum_{i\in\mathcal S}\delta_{3\mathbf e_i},$$ where $\mathbf e_i$ is the coordinate unit vector and, for every bounded measurable $f$, $$\label{fnd:operator}
 \int f\,d\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}(Q)
 =\sum_{n\geq0}\mathbb P(D=n)
   \int Z f(m^{\mathrm{out}})\,dQ^{\otimes n}.$$ In this formula Equation <a href="#fnd:product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:product">[fnd:product]</a> is applied to $m_{i,j}=1+\lambda(\widetilde m_{i,j}-1)$, where $\widetilde m_{\cdot,j}$ are the independent vectors sampled from $Q$. Each conditional integral has total mass one. The operator preserves balanced laws and permutation symmetry.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fnd-recursion"><summary>Proof of stmt-fnd-recursion</summary>

*Proof.* Conditional on the offspring count and the parent spin, the child subtree observations are independent copies of the depth-$\ell$ experiment passed through one edge. Equations <a href="#fnd:edge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:edge">[fnd:edge]</a> and <a href="#fnd:product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:product">[fnd:product]</a> give their combination. The count is observed and independent of the parent spin, so it is mixed with its original law in Equation <a href="#fnd:operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:operator">[fnd:operator]</a>. For each $i$, the expectation of the output coordinate is $\int W_i\,dQ^{\otimes n}=1$, proving balance. Relabelling the input coordinates commutes with every operation. At depth zero the root itself is observed, giving $Q_0$. Random subtree shapes are already included in each child datum; no additional conditioning or averaging convention is required. ◻

</details>

<a id="fnd:conditional-jensen"></a>
We say that one experiment is *degraded* from another when its datum is obtained from the latter by a random kernel independent of the input spin. If $Y'$ is degraded from $Y$, their posterior likelihoods satisfy $$\label{fnd:conditional-jensen}
 m(Y')=\mathbb E[m(Y)\mid Y'],\qquad
 \mathbb E\Phi(m(Y'))\leq\mathbb E\Phi(m(Y))$$ for every integrable convex function $\Phi$ of the posterior vector. This is the tower property followed by conditional Jensen. In particular it applies to total variation, $x$, and $x^2$: total variation is the convex function $\max_i|m_i-1|/3$, while $\zeta$ is linear in $m$ and its squared and fourth powers of the modulus are convex. Applying degradation kernels separately to conditionally independent branches shows that edge transmission and branch composition preserve this order.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Convergence, fixed points, and extinction

<a id="stmt-fnd-limit"></a>
<div id="fnd:limit" class="proposition">

<a id="fnd:fixed-point"></a>
**Proposition 6** (The limiting posterior). *On a common realization of the tree and spins, the root posterior likelihood vectors $m^{(\ell)}$ converge almost surely to a vector $m^{(\infty)}$. Their laws converge weakly to a symmetric balanced law $Q_\infty$ satisfying $$\label{fnd:fixed-point}
 Q_\infty=\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}(Q_\infty).$$ The advantages $a_\ell$ and moments $\mu_\ell=\mathbb Ex_\ell$ are nonincreasing and converge to their corresponding expectations under $Q_\infty$. This holds throughout $-1/2\leq\lambda\leq1$, including parameters for which edge probabilities vanish.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fnd-limit"><summary>Proof of stmt-fnd-limit</summary>

*Proof.* The observations $O_\ell$ themselves do not generate a decreasing sequence of sigma-fields. Instead put $$\mathcal F_\ell=\sigma(O_k:k\geq\ell).$$ Given $O_\ell$, the shapes and spins below its boundary are generated independently of the root spin. This statement includes every finite collection of subsequent depth observations, and hence their generated sigma-field. Therefore $$\mathbb E[\mathbf1_{\{S_\rho=i\}}\mid\mathcal F_\ell]
 =\mathbb P(S_\rho=i\mid O_\ell)=\tfrac13m_i^{(\ell)}.$$ The reverse martingale convergence theorem, applied to these bounded indicators and the decreasing fields $\mathcal F_\ell$, gives almost-sure and $L^1$ convergence. The limit is the root posterior given $\bigcap_\ell\mathcal F_\ell$. Every continuous function of the posterior converges in expectation by bounded convergence on the compact simplex. Moreover $m^{(\ell+1)}=\mathbb E[m^{(\ell)}\mid\mathcal F_{\ell+1}]$, so conditional Jensen proves the stated monotonicity. Lemma <a href="#fnd:ordered" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:ordered">3</a> transfers these conclusions to the required unlabelled observations.

It remains to pass Equation <a href="#fnd:law-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:law-recursion">[fnd:law-recursion]</a> to the limit. For a fixed count $n$ and a bounded continuous $f$, the integrand $Zf(m^{\mathrm{out}})$ in Equation <a href="#fnd:operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:operator">[fnd:operator]</a> is continuous where $Z>0$. Give it the value zero where $Z=0$. This extension is continuous, since $$|Zf(m^{\mathrm{out}})|\leq\|f\|_\infty Z$$ and $Z$ is continuous and nonnegative. Weak convergence of the finite product laws therefore gives convergence of the integral for each fixed $n$, even at the coloring endpoint. The tail of the count mixture is uniformly bounded by <a id="fnd:count-tail"></a>$$\label{fnd:count-tail}
 \left|\sum_{n>M}\mathbb P(D=n)
     \int Zf(m^{\mathrm{out}})\,dQ^{\otimes n}\right|
 \leq\|f\|_\infty\mathbb P(D>M),$$ because each conditional tilted law has mass one. First truncate the count, then pass to the weak limit, and finally let $M\to\infty$. Thus $\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}$ is weakly continuous on balanced laws. Since both $Q_\ell$ and $Q_{\ell+1}$ converge to $Q_\infty$, Equation <a href="#fnd:fixed-point" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:fixed-point">[fnd:fixed-point]</a> follows. ◻

</details>

Unless a different experiment is specified, we henceforth use <a id="fnd:limit-moments"></a>$$\label{fnd:limit-moments}
 \mu=\mathbb Ex,\qquad \nu=\mathbb Ex^2,\qquad \eta=\mathbb Ey$$ for the limiting experiment. Its law is trivial precisely when $\mu=0$, since $x=0$ is equivalent to $m=(1,1,1)$. By Lemma <a href="#fnd:norms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:norms">4</a> and Proposition <a href="#fnd:limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:limit">6</a>, non-reconstruction is equivalent to $\mu=0$.

<a id="stmt-fnd-log-integrability"></a>
<div id="fnd:log-integrability" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Logarithms at interior channel parameters). *If $-1/2<\lambda<1$, every fixed point of $\mathcal R_{\lambda,D}$ has strictly positive coordinates almost surely. Every polynomial in its coordinates and their logarithms is integrable under either offspring law in Equation <a href="#fnd:offspring" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:offspring">[fnd:offspring]</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fnd-log-integrability"><summary>Proof of stmt-fnd-log-integrability</summary>

*Proof.* For every $m\in\mathcal M$, its edge coordinates lie in $[c_-,c_+]$, where $$c_- =\min(1-\lambda,1+2\lambda)>0,\qquad
 c_+ =\max(1-\lambda,1+2\lambda).$$ At count $n$, both $W_i$ and $Z$ lie in $[c_-^n,c_+^n]$. Hence <a id="fnd:log-bound"></a>$$\label{fnd:log-bound}
 |\log m_i^{\mathrm{out}}|\leq n\log(c_+/c_-).$$ The count has its original law in the tilted output construction, and either specified law has finite moments of every order. The coordinates themselves lie in $[0,3]$. The bound proves the assertion, and also bounds each centered log coordinate $\log m_i-\langle\log m\rangle$ by $2n\log(c_+/c_-)$. At count zero all these logarithms are zero. No claim of this kind is needed at $\lambda=-1/2$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-fnd-survival"></a>
<div id="fnd:survival" class="lemma">

<a id="fnd:survival-equation"></a>
**Lemma 8** (The extinction factor). *Let $s=1$ in the regular model and let $s$ be the survival probability in the Poisson model. For the limiting experiment, <a id="fnd:survival-moments"></a>$$\label{fnd:survival-moments}
 \mu^2\leq s\nu,\qquad |\eta|\leq\sqrt{\mu\nu}.$$ In the Poisson model with $d>1$, $0<s<1$ and $$\label{fnd:survival-equation}
 -\log(1-s)=ds,\qquad
 s\leq2(1-1/d).$$ In particular, at $d\lambda^2=1$, $s\leq2(1-\lambda^2)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fnd-survival"><summary>Proof of stmt-fnd-survival</summary>

*Proof.* On extinction the boundary is empty at all sufficiently large depths. An observation with empty boundary consists only of a tree shape, which is independent of the root spin; its posterior is uniform. Thus $x_\infty=0$ on extinction. Cauchy–Schwarz gives $\mu=\mathbb E[x_\infty\mathbf1_{\{\mathrm{survival}\}}]
\leq\sqrt{s\nu}$. Also $|\eta|\leq\mathbb Ex^{3/2}\leq\sqrt{\mu\nu}$ by Equation <a href="#fnd:invariant-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:invariant-bounds">[fnd:invariant-bounds]</a>.

For completeness, the extinction probability is the increasing limit of the probabilities of an empty generation. The offspring generating function gives it as the smallest solution $q\in[0,1]$ of $q=\exp(d(q-1))$. Strict convexity and the derivative $d>1$ at $q=1$ show that this smallest solution belongs to $(0,1)$. Setting $q=1-s$ proves the first identity in Equation <a href="#fnd:survival-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:survival-equation">[fnd:survival-equation]</a>. For $0<s<1$, $$-\log(1-s)=\sum_{k\geq1}\frac{s^k}{k}
 \geq\sum_{k\geq1}\frac{s^k}{2^{k-1}}
 =\frac{s}{1-s/2},$$ where $2^{k-1}\geq k$. Dividing by $s$ and using the survival equation gives $d\geq(1-s/2)^{-1}$, equivalently the second inequality in Equation <a href="#fnd:survival-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:survival-equation">[fnd:survival-equation]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Reconstruction above the second-moment threshold

<a id="stmt-fnd-supercritical"></a>
<div id="fnd:supercritical" class="proposition">

**Proposition 9** (A linear estimator). *In either model, $d\lambda^2>1$ implies reconstruction, for either sign of $\lambda$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fnd-supercritical"><summary>Proof of stmt-fnd-supercritical</summary>

*Proof.* The hypothesis implies $\lambda\neq0$. Define the observable, order-independent estimator <a id="fnd:linear-estimator"></a>$$\label{fnd:linear-estimator}
 L_\ell=(d\lambda)^{-\ell}\sum_{|u|=\ell}S_u,
 \qquad L_0=S_\rho.$$ The sum is zero on an empty boundary. Since $\mathbb E[S_{\mathrm{child}}\mid S_{\mathrm{parent}}]
=\lambda S_{\mathrm{parent}}$, induction gives $\mathbb E[L_\ell\mid S_\rho]=S_\rho$.

Let $L_\ell^{(j)}$ denote the corresponding estimator in the subtree rooted at child $j$. Then $L_{\ell+1}=(d\lambda)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^D L_\ell^{(j)}$. Conditional on $S_\rho$ and $D$, these variables are independent, and each has mean $\lambda S_\rho$. The diagonal terms in the squared modulus sum to $d\mathbb E|L_\ell|^2$; each ordered off-diagonal pair has expectation $\lambda^2$. Consequently, with $q=(d\lambda^2)^{-1}<1$, <a id="fnd:estimator-recurrence"></a>$$\label{fnd:estimator-recurrence}
 \mathbb E|L_{\ell+1}|^2
 =q\mathbb E|L_\ell|^2+\alpha,\qquad
 \mathbb E|L_\ell|^2=q^\ell+\alpha\frac{1-q^\ell}{1-q}
 \leq1+\frac{\alpha}{1-q}=:C.$$ The factor $\alpha$ is the normalized factorial moment, rather than the normalized second moment, because it counts distinct branches.

The correlation with the root equals one. Conditioning on the observed experiment and applying Cauchy–Schwarz gives $$1=\mathbb E[L_\ell\overline{S_\rho}]
   =\mathbb E[L_\ell\overline{\zeta_\ell}],\qquad
 1\leq\mathbb E|L_\ell|^2\,\mathbb E|\zeta_\ell|^2.$$ Thus $\mu_\ell\geq C^{-1}$ at every depth, and Equation <a href="#fnd:tv-moment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:tv-moment">[fnd:tv-moment]</a> gives $a_\ell\geq(\sqrt3 C)^{-1}>0$. The limiting advantage exists by Proposition <a href="#fnd:limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:limit">6</a>, so it is positive. In particular, this argument uses the original Poisson experiment, without conditioning on survival. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Decreasing the channel strength or the offspring mean

<a id="stmt-fnd-degradation"></a>
<div id="fnd:degradation" class="proposition">

**Proposition 10** (Channel and offspring degradation). *At every finite depth, each of the following changes produces a degraded root experiment:*

1.  *replacing $\lambda$ by $c\lambda$, where $0\leq c\leq1$;*

2.  *replacing a regular offspring count $b_+$ by an integer $2\leq b_-\leq b_+$;*

3.  *replacing a Poisson offspring mean $d_+$ by $0<d_-\leq d_+$.*

*Consequently each change can only decrease the expected total variation advantage and the moments $\mathbb Ex$ and $\mathbb Ex^2$, both at finite depth and in their limits.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fnd-degradation"><summary>Proof of stmt-fnd-degradation</summary>

*Proof.* The channel admits a group-increment construction. On each edge $e$ take an independent $G_e\in\mathcal S$ with $$\mathbb P(G_e=1)=\frac{1+2\lambda}{3},\qquad
 \mathbb P(G_e=\omega)=\mathbb P(G_e=\omega^2)=\frac{1-\lambda}{3},$$ and set $S_v=S_\rho\prod_{e\in[\rho,v]}G_e$. The increment law is invariant under conjugation and has mean $\mathbb EG_e=\lambda$. Independently generate increments $H_e$ with the same form of law at parameter $c$. A conjugation-invariant probability law on $\mathcal S$ is uniquely determined by its real mean: if its mass at 1 is $p$, that mean is $(3p-1)/2$. The independent products $G_eH_e$ are conjugation-invariant and have mean $c\lambda$, so they have exactly the channel increment law for $c\lambda$.

Given only the observed tree through depth $\ell$ and its boundary spins, generate the $H_e$’s on its edges and replace each boundary spin by $$S'_v=S_v\prod_{e\in[\rho,v]}H_e.$$ This kernel requires neither the root spin nor any unobserved internal spins, and the output has exactly the depth experiment for parameter $c\lambda$. Shared path increments give the required joint law of the transformed boundary, not merely its individual marginals.

For regular trees, in the ordered representation retain the first $b_-$ children at each retained vertex and discard the rest of their subtrees. The retained boundary and tree have the smaller regular experiment. For Poisson trees, independently keep each child of a retained vertex with probability $p=d_-/d_+$. If $D_+$ is its original count and $D_-$ its retained count, then $$\mathbb Ez^{D_-}=\mathbb E(1-p+pz)^{D_+}
             =\exp(d_-(z-1)).$$ Independence across vertices and independence from the edge increments show that the retained observation has the Poisson broadcast law with mean $d_-$. These operations are performed only on the known tree and observed boundary; neither requires a survival event.

By Lemma <a href="#fnd:ordered" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:ordered">3</a>, an unlabelled observation can first be randomly ordered without accessing the root, and the final ordering can then be forgotten. All the preceding kernels are therefore valid for the unlabelled model as well. Their comparison of the stated quantities follows from Equation <a href="#fnd:conditional-jensen" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fnd:conditional-jensen">[fnd:conditional-jensen]</a>, and passes to the limits by Proposition <a href="#fnd:limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:limit">6</a>. ◻

</details>

At $\lambda=0$, every positive-depth boundary is independent of the root conditional on the tree, so the advantage is zero. For each fixed $d$, Proposition <a href="#fnd:degradation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:degradation">10</a> reduces the remaining subcritical channel parameters of either sign to their admissible equality parameters. Negative equality is admissible exactly when $d\geq4$; for smaller branching parameters, non-reconstruction at the coloring channel $\lambda=-1/2$ and branching parameter four will imply all the needed negative-channel statements by the same proposition.

<a id="sec:positive"></a>
### Positive channels at equality

We prove that the limiting posterior is uniform when $0<\lambda<1$ and $d\lambda^2=1$. Throughout this section, $m$ has the limiting fixed-point law supplied by Proposition <a href="#fnd:limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fnd:limit">6</a>, $v=m-1$, and $$\mu=\mathbb Ex,\qquad \nu=\mathbb Ex^2,\qquad \eta=\mathbb Ey,\qquad
